# API field assertions — Postman-style checks on every journey request

Generated by `prototype/scripts/build-api-assertions.ts` from the responses real API journeys captured. Each case is one assertion the journey runner evaluates **live on every run** (`prototype/src/checks/apiAssertions.ts`) — the report shows it under the step as passed / failed / not checked. Required = present in every captured response; a format is claimed only when the field name names it and every observed value conforms. Do not edit by hand — re-run the script.

_Built 2026-09-30T11:02:31.536Z from 36 captured responses across 2 run(s)._

## 1. Sign in as the buyer — `GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`

Step `buyer_login` (buyer) · Postman: auth › generate otp, verify otp · seen in: RFQ only, Technical + RFQ · 2 sample(s).

#### API-FBLOGIN-001 — Sign in as the buyer succeeds
- **Why it matters.** Every later check reads a success body; a refusal here is the headline, not a field detail.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical + RFQ), at the step "Sign in as the buyer".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Sign in as the buyer” in the API journey (`GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`). 2. Note how the app answers.
- **Expected.** The app accepts the request and answers with a success.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `status_2xx`, learned from 2 real response(s).
- **Trace.** P0 · automate · `apiAssertions.ts` · `buyer_login`

#### API-FBLOGIN-002 — Sign in as the buyer answers within 7 seconds
- **Why it matters.** A regression that makes a core call several times slower is a defect even when the body is right.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical + RFQ), at the step "Sign in as the buyer".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Sign in as the buyer” in the API journey (`GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`). 2. Time the answer.
- **Expected.** The app answers within 7 seconds.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `max_ms 7000`, learned from 2 real response(s).
- **Trace.** P2 · automate · `apiAssertions.ts` · `buyer_login`

#### API-FBLOGIN-003 — Sign in as the buyer returns readable data
- **Why it matters.** Every field check assumes a parsed body; an HTML error page with a 200 would otherwise pass silently.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical + RFQ), at the step "Sign in as the buyer".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Sign in as the buyer” in the API journey (`GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`). 2. Look at what comes back.
- **Expected.** The app answers with structured data (JSON), not an error page or blank reply.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `json_body`, learned from 2 real response(s).
- **Trace.** P1 · automate · `apiAssertions.ts` · `buyer_login`

#### API-FBLOGIN-052 — The user's status is returned
- **Why it matters.** It was present in all 2 real response(s); a client reading it would break if it vanished.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical + RFQ), at the step "Sign in as the buyer".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Sign in as the buyer” in the API journey (`GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`). 2. Look at the user's status in the app's response.
- **Expected.** The response includes the user's status.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `exists user.status`, learned from 2 real response(s).
- **Trace.** P0 · automate · `apiAssertions.ts` · `buyer_login`

#### API-FBLOGIN-053 — The user's status is text
- **Why it matters.** A type change (a number turning into a string, an object into an array) breaks every consumer silently.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical + RFQ), at the step "Sign in as the buyer".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Sign in as the buyer” in the API journey (`GET /v1/dashboard_configuration`). 2. Look at the user's status in the app's response.
- **Expected.** The user's status is text.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `type_is user.status ["string"]`, learned from 2 real response(s).
- **Trace.** P1 · automate · `apiAssertions.ts` · `buyer_login`

#### API-FDRAFT-004 — The new event's status is saved exactly as sent
- **Why it matters.** The server stored what the buyer sent — the core promise of a create or update, and what a Postman test on the echo proves.
- **Setup.** A buyer walking the API journey (RFQ only, Technical + RFQ), at the step "Create the draft event".
- **Steps.** 1. As the buyer, do “Create the draft event” in the API journey (`POST /event_groups`), noting the value sent for it. 2. Compare the new event's status in the app's response with what was sent.
- **Expected.** The new event's status is exactly what the buyer sent.
- **Oracle.** Checked automatically on every API journey run; the report shows what actually came back. Engine check: `equals_request status ← status`, learned from 2 real response(s).
